"<function_result call_id=\"1\">\ndocs/PRD.md:\n# Goodnumbers Weekly Health Journal PRD\n\n**Revision:** v0.7 (Codebase-aligned)\n\nGoodNumbers is a weekly health journal that is a combination of a diary/bullet journal, statistical analyzer and AI coach.\n\n**Problem:** Type 1 Diabetics typically see their specialists quarterly or less, making it challenging to identify and address daily and weekly trends in their blood glucose management.\n\n**Solution:** GoodNumbers provides a weekly practice of self-reflection, empowering Type 1 Diabetics to proactively manage their health. It leverages:\n\n- **Data Analysis:** To help users find \"hotspots\" and trends in their blood glucose numbers.\n- **AI Coaching:** To help users consider behavioral or treatment changes (always after consulting with a doctor).\n- **Personalized Podcast:** For novelty and a new way to explain data, offering a weekly audio summary of their progress and wins, making the self-reflection process feel positive and motivating, like a beneficial workout.\n\nThe goal of GoodNumbers is to give Type 1 Diabetics a weekly practice of self-reflection, including:\n\n- A pause in the week to look back over the last week, see how they are feeling, and celebrate wins and places to improve.\n- Reviewing trends, ‘problem areas’ and ‘hotspots’ in their blood glucose numbers (e.g., highs and lows). GoodNumbers will provide statistical analyses to take the work out of finding the problem areas and hotspots in their blood glucose management.\n- Use AI to take in their personal experiences, correlate to the data, and provide a report (in the form of text/charts as well as a personal podcast) so they can improve for the next week.\n\nGoodNumbers is meant to be motivating - there is no judgement - just recognition of a hard job well done (managing blood glucose) and providing real data and feedback on how to improve.\n\nIt should be noted that GoodNumbers does NOT provide medical advice - rather, it recognizes patterns and makes /general/ recommendations that the patient should then take to their doctor or medical healthcare team.\n\n## UX Design System\n\nThis section outlines the core principles of the GoodNumbers visual identity and user interface components. The design is intended to be clean, professional, calm, and supportive, creating a trustworthy environment for users to reflect on their health data.\n\n### Overall Philosophy\n\n- **Aesthetic:** Modern, clean, and uncluttered. The interface prioritizes readability and ease of use, with a professional feel that inspires confidence.\n- **Layout:** The system is built on a card-based architecture, using an off-white background (`#F4F1EA`) to create a soft, inviting canvas. Components have rounded corners and use subtle shadows sparingly to create a sense of depth and organization without adding visual noise.\n- **Theming:** The color system is designed with CSS variables to facilitate the future implementation of a dark mode.\n\n### Color Palette\n\nThe palette is unified and intentional, using a \"Mesa\" theme (Terracotta and Petrol Blue) to create a warm, distinctive, and supportive atmosphere.\n\n- **Primary Color (Mesa Primary - Terracotta):** Used for primary actions, buttons, and key highlights. It is warm and inviting.\n  - `--color-mesa-primary: #D9775B`\n\n- **Secondary Color (Mesa Secondary - Petrol Blue):** Used for brand headers, stability indicators, and secondary accents.\n  - `--color-mesa-secondary: #2C4C5B`\n\n- **Critical Alert Color (Red):** Reserved exclusively for high-priority warnings to ensure its significance is not diluted. Its primary use is for the site-wide medical disclaimer banner.\n  - `--feedback-critical-color: #D32F2F`\n\n- **Neutral Colors:** A palette of grays and off-whites forms the foundation of the interface.\n  - `--color-mesa-bg: #F4F1EA` (Main page background - a warm off-white)\n  - `--color-mesa-surface: #FFFFFF` (Card backgrounds)\n  - `--color-mesa-text: #1F2937` (Primary text)\n  - `--color-mesa-muted: #9CA3AF` (Secondary text)\n  - `--color-mesa-border: #E5E7EB` (Dividers and borders)\n\n### Typography\n\n- **Font Stack:** The application uses `Nunito` for UI elements (sans-serif) and `Lora` for narrative text (serif), creating a journal-like feel.\n\n### Core Components & Styling\n\n- **Banner Component:** The primary site banner uses a solid red (`--feedback-critical-color`) background with high-contrast white text. This makes the medical disclaimer impossible to miss.\n- **Buttons:**\n  - **Primary:** Solid Terracotta background with white text. Used for main calls-to-action (e.g., \"Login\", \"Start Journal\").\n  - **Secondary:** Transparent background with border and text. Used for less prominent actions.\n- **Cards:** Content is organized into cards with a white background, large rounded corners (`12px`), and a light box shadow. This separates information into clean, manageable chunks.\n\n## Home page\n\n### User Journey and Purpose\n\nThe homepage's primary purpose is new user acquisition and discovery. It serves as the initial touchpoint for potential users to understand GoodNumbers, its value proposition, and to initiate their journey either through a demo or by registering/logging in.\n\n### Standard Page Elements\n\n- **Top Right Button:** A \"Login / Register\" button should be prominently displayed in the top right corner of the homepage. This button leads to the [Login page](#login-page).\n- **Footer:** Located at the bottom, containing:\n  - Copyright information\n  - Links to Privacy Policy\n  - Links to Terms of Service\n\n## Banner component\n\nThe banner component is a reusable component. The title gets a special UI treatment.\n\n## Email Allowlist Access Control\n\nThis section defines the access control mechanism for the private beta/pre-release phase.\n\n1. Overview & Goal\n\n- Goal: To restrict access to the application to a pre-approved list of users.\n- Functionality: The system maintains a server-side list of allowed email addresses. Access is checked during the Google OAuth sign-in process.\n\n2. Interaction Flow\n\n  1. User clicks \"Sign in with Google\".\n  2. User authenticates with Google.\n  3. **Allowlist Check:** The system checks if the user's email is in the allowlist.\n  4. **Allowed:** The user proceeds to the Agreements or Dashboard page.\n  5. **Denied:** The sign-in fails, and the user is denied access.\n\n## Login page {#login-page}\n\n### 2. Goals\n\n- **Enable Secure User Access:** Provide a robust and secure mechanism for users to log into their existing accounts.\n- **Facilitate Seamless Onboarding:** Allow new users to easily register and gain access to the application with minimal friction, leveraging Google's identity.\n\n### 3. User Stories\n\n#### New User Registration\n\n- As a **new user**, I want to register quickly using my existing Google account.\n- As a **new user**, I want to understand and explicitly agree to the terms, privacy policy, and software disclaimer before I register.\n\n### 4. Functional Requirements\n\n#### 4.1. Authentication Interface\n\n- The authentication interface MUST primarily leverage Auth.js's built-in pages or endpoints.\n- The UI MUST clearly indicate that Google is the primary (and only) sign-in method.\n\n#### 4.2. Google OAuth Integration (Primary Authentication)\n\n- Users MUST be able to register and log in using their Google account.\n- **New User Flow:**\n  1. User signs in with Google.\n  2. If allowed, they are redirected to the **Agreements Page**.\n  3. User MUST check boxes to agree to Terms and Privacy Policy.\n  4. User clicks \"Accept and Continue\".\n  5. User is redirected to the **Setup Page**.\n\n## Authenticated User Experience & Navigation\n\n### Dashboard Header\n\nUpon successful login, the Dashboard and all subsequent authenticated pages will feature a persistent header with the following elements:\n\n- **Settings Button:** A button that links to the user's account settings page (the \"Setup Account\" page).\n- **Logout Button:** A button that allows the user to explicitly log out.\n\n## Demo Page\n\nThe Demo Page serves as an initial touchpoint for potential users to understand GoodNumbers' value proposition without requiring registration.\n\n- **Content Source:** The Demo Page will utilize the standard journal page structure and components, populated with pre-generated data.\n- **Interactivity:** The Demo Page MUST be presented as a read-only version.\n\n## Setup Account\n\n- The first time a user logs in (after agreements), they are taken to the Setup page.\n  - Nightscout URL (text field)\n  - Nightscout Token (password field)\n  - Preferred Units (mg/dL or mmol/L)\n- **Connection Flow:**\n  - The user enters credentials and clicks \"Save\".\n  - The system validates the input format and saves the encrypted credentials.\n\n## Dashboard\n\n- Upon successful login, the dashboard is the landing page for all authenticated users.\n\n### \"Log this week's journal\" Card (Primary Action)\n\n- **Functionality:** The \"Start Journal\" button initiates the background generation process.\n- **Visual Hierarchy:** This card is styled as a \"hero\" component.\n\n### \"Past weeks\" Section (Historical Journals)\n\n- **Ordering:** Historical Journals are shown in reverse chronological order (newest first).\n- **Card Layout:** Displays date, title, and description with a \"View\" and \"Delete\" button.\n\n## This week's journal\n\n### Journal Generation Process\n\nThis section describes the process that occurs after a user clicks \"Start Journal\".\n\n#### User Experience (Loading Screen)\n\nUpon clicking \"Start Journal,\" the user is navigated to a dedicated loading page that displays a progress bar and descriptive text to keep them informed. It polls the backend for status updates.\n\n#### Backend Process\n\n- **Data Fetching:** Fetches last 7 days of entries, treatments, and profile from Nightscout.\n- **Analysis:** Calculates AGP metrics (GMI, Variability, Time in Range) and Scorecard metrics.\n- **Hotspot Detection:** Identifies clusters of high/low events.\n- **Persistence:** Saves all data to the database.\n\n## Journal Page: UX/UI Concept\n\nThis page displays the generated report.\n\n#### Components\n\n- **Podcast Player:** (Placeholder/Future) For playing the AI-generated audio summary.\n- **Scorecards:** Displays key metrics (Avg Glucose, Stability, TIR) with trend indicators.\n- **AGP Chart:** Interactive chart showing median and percentile ranges.\n- **Chart Analysis:** AI-generated insights (placeholder/mocked in MVP if AI not active).\n- **Weekly Vibe:** Subjective tracking with emoji options (🥀 Wilted, 🌱 Sprouting, 🌿 Growing, 🌻 Flourishing).\n- **Influencing Factors:** Multi-select chips organized by category:\n  - **Food:** Heavy/Fatty Meal, Carb Error, Eating Out, Alcohol.\n  - **Movement:** Strenuous Exercise, Sedentary.\n  - **Body & Meds:** Illness, Hormonal, Dehydration, Set Change, Steroids.\n  - **Mind & Mood:** Stress, Anxiety, Sleep Quality.\n  - **Life & Tech:** Travel, System Malfunction.\n- **Event Cluster Cards:** Detailed view of detected glycemic patterns. Includes an interactive chart that visualizes **both glucose and treatment/carb data** to help identify causes.\n- **Goals:** Text area for next week's goals.\n- **Sticky Action Bar:** For saving changes.\n\n\n---\ndocs/TECHNICAL_SPECIFICATION.md:\n# Technical Specification: Goodnumbers Weekly Health Journal\n\n**Version:** 1.3 (Updated post-implementation)\n**Date:** 2025-10-23\n**Status:** Revised to reflect current codebase\n\n## 1. Introduction\n\nThis document provides a comprehensive technical specification for the Goodnumbers application. Goodnumbers is an experimental weekly health journal designed to help Type 1 Diabetics reflect on and improve their blood glucose management. It combines statistical analysis of CGM data with structured insights to create a supportive and motivating user experience.\n\n## 2. Requirements\n\n### 2.1. Functional Requirements\n\n#### 2.1.1. User Authentication (Auth.js)\n\n- **Access Control:** Access is restricted via an **email allowlist**. Only users whose Google account email is on a server-side list can successfully sign in.\n- **Provider:** Google OAuth is the sole authentication method.\n- **UI:** Auth.js built-in pages are used.\n- **Onboarding Flow:**\n  1.  User signs in with Google.\n  2.  **Agreements:** User must sign terms and privacy policy (`agreementsSigned` flag).\n  3.  **Setup:** User must provide Nightscout credentials (`nightscoutUrl`).\n\n#### 2.1.2. Journal Generation\n\n- **Trigger:** User clicks \"Start Journal\" on the Dashboard.\n- **Process:** A background job (BullMQ) is enqueued.\n- **Worker Logic:**\n  1.  Fetch 7 days of entries, treatments, and profile from Nightscout.\n  2.  Calculate AGP metrics (Median, Percentiles).\n  3.  Calculate Voyager Scorecard metrics (Avg Glucose, Stability, Time in Range, Time in Tight Range).\n  4.  Detect \"Hotspots\" (clusters of glycemic events) using the `HotspotDetector` engine.\n  5.  Persist all data to the database.\n\n## 3. Architecture\n\n- **Web Server:** Express.js.\n- **Background Worker:** Node.js process managed by BullMQ.\n- **Queue:** Redis.\n- **Database:** SQLite with Prisma ORM.\n- **Shared Packages:** Strictly layered unidirectional flow:\n  - `@goodnumbers/types`: Pure TS interfaces/enums (Zero dependencies).\n  - `@goodnumbers/schemas`: Zod validation definitions (Depends on `types`).\n  - `@goodnumbers/common`: Shared logic and utilities (Depends on `schemas` and `types`).\n\n## 4. Data Handling\n\n### 4.1. Prisma Schema\n\n```prisma\nmodel User {\n  id                   String    @id @default(cuid())\n  name                 String?\n  email                String?   @unique\n  emailVerified        DateTime?\n  image                String?\n  accounts             Account[]\n  sessions             Session[]\n  journals             Journal[]\n\n  // Onboarding & Settings\n  agreementsSigned     Boolean   @default(false)\n  nightscoutUrl        String?\n  nightscoutToken      String?   // Encrypted\n  nightscoutTokenLast3 String?   // Hint for UI\n  preferredUnits       GlucoseUnit @default(MGDL)\n  rssToken             String    @unique @default(cuid())\n}\n\nmodel Journal {\n  id                   String    @id @default(cuid())\n  createdAt            DateTime  @default(now())\n  updatedAt            DateTime  @updatedAt\n  userId               String\n  user                 User      @relation(fields: [userId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  // Job progress tracking\n  status               String    @default(\"PENDING\")\n  progress             Int       @default(0)\n  statusMessage        String?\n\n  // User-provided subjective inputs\n  weeklyVibe           String?\n  influencingFactors   Json?\n  goalsForNextWeek     String?\n\n  // AI & Analysis Content\n  podcastTitle         String?\n  podcastDescription   String?\n  podcastAudioUrl      String?\n  agpChartData         Json?\n  analysisInsights     Json?\n  treatments           Json?     // Stored treatments for the week\n  scoreCardData        Json?     // Voyager metrics (TIR, GMI, etc.)\n\n  // Relation to detailed analysis\n  clusters             GlycemicEventCluster[]\n}\n\nmodel GlycemicEventCluster {\n  id                  String    @id @default(cuid())\n  journalId           String\n  journal             Journal   @relation(fields: [journalId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  eventType           String\n  eventCount          Int\n  meanTimeMinutes     Int\n  clusterDataJson     Json      // Full cluster data for charts\n  userNotes           String?\n}\n```\n\n### 4.2. Flexible JSON Fields\n\n- `agpChartData`: Array of `AgpDataPoint` objects.\n- `scoreCardData`: Object containing `avgGlucose`, `stability`, `timeInRange`, `timeInTightRange` and `trends`.\n- `treatments`: Array of normalized treatment objects (carbs, insulin).\n\n## 5. API Design\n\nAll endpoints require authentication (`protect`) and CSRF protection.\n\n- **`GET /api/csrf-token`**: Returns a CSRF token.\n\n- **`POST /api/journals`**: Enqueues a new journal generation job.\n- **`GET /api/journals/:id/status`**: Polls the status of a job.\n- **`GET /api/journals`**: Lists journal summaries.\n- **`GET /api/journals/:id`**: Fetches full journal data.\n- **`PUT /api/journals/:id`**: Updates user inputs (vibe, goals, notes).\n- **`DELETE /api/journals/:id`**: Deletes a journal.\n\n- **`PUT /api/user/settings`**: Updates Nightscout credentials and units.\n  - Note: `nightscoutToken` is encrypted before storage. `nightscoutTokenLast3` is stored as a hint.\n\n## 6. Security Measures\n\n- **Encryption:** `nightscoutToken` is encrypted at rest using AES-256-GCM (`backend/src/lib/encryption.ts`).\n- **CSRF:** Implemented via `tiny-csrf` with a token endpoint.\n- **Rate Limiting:** Applied to all API routes, with stricter limits on `POST /journals` and `PUT /settings`.\n- **Input Validation:** Zod 4.3.5 schemas used for all API inputs (shared via `@goodnumbers/schemas`). Version is pinned monorepo-wide to ensure type safety.\n- **Package Security:** All shared packages are marked `private: true` to prevent dependency confusion attacks.\n- **Data Segregation:** All DB queries filter by `userId`.\n\n## 7. Future Work / Known Limitations\n\n- **AI/Podcast:** The schema supports AI-generated content (`podcastAudioUrl`, `analysisInsights`), but the backend integration with Gemini/TTS is currently a placeholder or pending implementation in the worker.\n- **RSS Token:** The `rssToken` field exists on the User model, but the endpoint to regenerate it (`POST /api/user/regenerate-rss-token`) is not yet implemented.\n\n\n---\ndocs/DEVELOPMENT_PROCESS.md:\n# Goodnumbers Development Process\n\n**Version:** 2.0 (Monorepo Update)\n**Date:** 2025-10-23\n\n## 1. Overview\n\nThis document outlines the standardized Git workflow and development process for the Goodnumbers project. Adhering to this process ensures a stable codebase, a clean and understandable version history, and a predictable, quality-driven development cycle. The core of our strategy is a **Feature Branch Workflow** with **Conventional Commits**, targeting a central `develop` integration branch.\n\n## 2. Branching Strategy\n\nOur branching model is designed for clarity and stability, centered around a primary development branch and short-lived feature branches.\n\n### 2.1. Core Branches\n\n- **`main`:** This branch represents the official, production-ready release history. It should only be updated from the `develop` branch during a formal release process. Direct commits to `main` are strictly forbidden.\n\n- **`develop`:** This is the primary integration branch for day-to-day development. It contains the latest delivered development changes for the next release. It must always compile and pass all tests, but it is not considered production-stable until formally promoted to `main`.\n\n### 2.2. Feature Branches\n\nAll new work, including features, bug fixes, and chores, must be done on a dedicated feature branch.\n\n- **Creation:** Always create a new feature branch from the latest version of `develop`.\n\n  ```bash\n  git checkout develop\n  git pull origin develop\n  git checkout -b <branch-name>\n  ```\n\n- **Naming Convention:** To maintain clarity, branches must be named using the format `type/short-description`. The `type` should align with our commit conventions (see below).\n  - **Examples:**\n    - `feat/database-schema`\n    - `test/add-encryption-utils`\n    - `fix/user-settings-endpoint`\n    - `chore/update-dependencies`\n    - `refactor/auth-middleware`\n\n## 3. Commit Strategy: Conventional Commits\n\nWe will adhere to the **Conventional Commits** specification for all commit messages. This creates a readable history and allows for potential automation of versioning and changelogs.\n\n### 3.1. Commit Message Format\n\n```\n<type>(<scope>): <subject>\n\n[optional body]\n\n[optional footer]\n```\n\n- **`<type>`:** Describes the kind of change.\n  - `feat`: A new feature.\n  - `fix`: A bug fix.\n  - `test`: Adding or correcting tests.\n  - `refactor`: A code change that neither fixes a bug nor adds a feature.\n  - `docs`: Documentation only changes.\n  - `style`: Changes that do not affect the meaning of the code (white-space, formatting, etc).\n  - `chore`: Changes to the build process, dependency updates, or other auxiliary tools.\n\n- **`<scope>` (optional):** The section of the codebase affected (e.g., `api`, `db`, `ui`, `auth`).\n\n- **`<subject>`:** A concise, imperative-mood description of the change (e.g., \"add health check endpoint\").\n\n### 3.2. Example Commits\n\n- `feat(db): implement initial prisma schema`\n- `test(server): add integration test for /health endpoint`\n- `fix(api): enforce ownership on journal deletion endpoint`\n\n## 4. The Pull Request (PR) Process\n\nThe Pull Request is the central mechanism for quality control. All code must be reviewed and automatically verified via a PR before it is integrated into the `develop` branch.\n\n### 4.1. Step-by-Step Workflow\n\n1.  **Branch:** Create your feature branch from the latest `develop`.\n2.  **Work & Commit:** Complete a single, discrete task from the implementation plan. Make small, logical commits on your branch using the Conventional Commit format.\n3.  **Push:** Push your completed feature branch to the remote repository.\n4.  **Open a Pull Request:** Open a PR to merge your feature branch into the **`develop`** branch.\n\n### 4.2. PR Quality Standards\n\n- **Title:** The PR title should be clear and follow the Conventional Commit format.\n- **Description:** The description must include:\n  - A brief summary of **what** was changed and **why**.\n  - A link to the related task or issue.\n  - A **\"How to Test\"** section explaining the steps a reviewer can take to verify the changes.\n- **Scope:** A PR should be small and focused, representing a single unit of work.\n\n### 4.3. Automated Quality Gates\n\nThe repository is configured with a CI/CD pipeline. You must run these checks locally before pushing:\n\n1.  **Linting:** `npm run lint` (runs across all workspaces).\n2.  **Type Checking:** `npm run build:backend` and `npm run build:frontend` (verifies types).\n3.  **Automated Tests:**\n    - Backend: `npm run test:backend`\n    - Frontend: `npm run test:frontend`\n\n**A PR must be blocked from merging if any of these checks fail.**\n\n### 4.4. Review and Merge Process\n\n1.  **Review:** A teammate must review and approve the PR.\n2.  **Merge:** Once the PR is approved and all automated checks have passed, it can be merged into `develop`.\n3.  **Merge Strategy:** Use **\"Squash and Merge\"**.\n4.  **Clean Up:** After merging, the feature branch should be deleted from the remote repository.\n\n### 4.5. Security Checks\n\nBefore a PR is merged, the author must perform the following security check:\n\n1.  **Dependency Audit:** Run `npm audit` from the project root. Any **high** or **critical** severity vulnerabilities must be addressed.\n\n\n---\ndocs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:\n# Goodnumbers Implementation Plan\n\n**Version:** 5.0 (Post-MVP Reconciliation)\n**Date:** 2025-10-23\n\n## 1. "<function_result call_id=\"1\">\nimport { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';\nimport * as fs from 'fs/promises';\nimport * as path from 'path';\n\ndescribe('Monorepo Configuration Integrity', () => {\n  // Paths relative to this test file\n  const rootDir = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../..');\n  const packagesDir = path.join(rootDir, 'packages');\n  const backendDir = path.resolve(__dirname, '../..');\n\n  it('should ensure all local packages have path mappings in backend tsconfig.json', async () => {\n    // 1. Get list of local packages in the workspace\n    const entries = await fs.readdir(packagesDir, { withFileTypes: true });\n    const packageNames = entries\n      .filter((dirent) => dirent.isDirectory())\n      .map((dirent) => dirent.name);\n\n    // 2. Read backend tsconfig\n    const tsconfigPath = path.join(backendDir, 'tsconfig.json');\n    const tsconfigContent = await fs.readFile(tsconfigPath, 'utf-8');\n    // Parse JSON (allowing for potential comments if standard JSON, but basic parse works for strict JSON)\n    const tsconfig = JSON.parse(tsconfigContent);\n    const paths = tsconfig.compilerOptions.paths || {};\n\n    // 3. Verify each package is mapped\n    packageNames.forEach((pkg) => {\n      const expectedPathKey = `@goodnumbers/${pkg}`;\n      expect(paths, `Missing \"paths\" entry in backend/tsconfig.json for ${expectedPathKey}. Please add it to resolve types during development.`).toHaveProperty(expectedPathKey);\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('should ensure all local packages have aliases in backend vitest.config.ts', async () => {\n    // 1. Get list of local packages\n    const entries = await fs.readdir(packagesDir, { withFileTypes: true });\n    const packageNames = entries\n      .filter((dirent) => dirent.isDirectory())\n      .map((dirent) => dirent.name);\n\n    // 2. Read backend vitest config (as text, since we can't easily require ts files here)\n    const vitestConfigPath = path.join(backendDir, 'vitest.config.ts');\n    const vitestConfigContent = await fs.readFile(vitestConfigPath, 'utf-8');\n\n    // 3. Verify each package is aliased\n    packageNames.forEach((pkg) => {\n      const expectedAlias = `'@goodnumbers/${pkg}'`;\n      expect(vitestConfigContent, `Missing alias in backend/vitest.config.ts for ${expectedAlias}. Please add it to resolve sources during testing.`).toContain(expectedAlias);\n    });\n  });\n});\n\n</function_result>"

"The errors indicate that the `backend` and `packages/schemas` are trying to import from `@goodnumbers/types`, but TypeScript (and the build process) cannot find the generated declaration files (`dist/index.d.ts`). This is causing `TS2307` (Cannot find module) and `TS6305` (Output file has not been built) errors.\n\nThe root cause is likely that the packages are not being built in the correct order, or the path mappings for development are missing in the root `tsconfig` or package-level configs, causing them to rely on the `dist` folder which might be empty or stale.\n\nIn a monorepo setup using \"Project References\" (indicated by `composite: true` in `packages/types/tsconfig.json` and `packages/schemas/tsconfig.json`), we have two ways to run:\n1.  **Build Mode**: Run `tsc -b` from the root, which builds dependencies in topological order.\n2.  **Dev Mode (Source)**: Use path mappings (`paths` in `tsconfig.json`) to point directly to `src/index.ts` so we don't need to rebuild on every change.\n\nThe backend `tsconfig.json` (read in previous turns) *does* have path mappings:\n```json\n    \"paths\": {\n      \"@src/*\": [\"src/*\"],\n      \"ioredis\": [\"./node_modules/ioredis/dist/index.d.ts\"],\n      \"@goodnumbers/common\": [\"../packages/common/src/index.ts\"],\n      \"@goodnumbers/schemas\": [\"../packages/schemas/src/index.ts\"],\n      \"@goodnumbers/types\": [\"../packages/types/src/index.ts\"]\n    }\n```\nHowever, the error `Output file ... has not been built from source file ...` typically happens when using Project References (`references`) *alongside* path mappings that point to source files, but the compiler expects the referenced project to be built because it sees the `references` array.\n\nThe backend `tsconfig.json` has:\n```json\n  \"references\": [\n    { \"path\": \"../packages/schemas\" },\n    { \"path\": \"../packages/types\" }\n  ]\n```\n\nWhen `references` are present, TypeScript expects the referenced projects to be built (generating `.d.ts` files in `dist`). If `paths` point to `src`, it can create a conflict or confusion if the `outDir` structure isn't perfectly aligned or if `tsc --build` isn't used.\n\n**The Fix Plan:**\n\n1.  **Fix `packages/types/package.json`**: The `exports` field is missing the `\"types\"` condition.\n    *   Current: `\"exports\": { \".\": { \"import\": \"./dist/index.js\", \"require\": \"./dist/index.js\" } }`\n    *   Fix: Add `\"types\": \"./dist/index.d.ts\"` to the export map.\n\n2.  **Fix `packages/schemas/package.json`**: Same issue, missing `\"types\"` in exports.\n\n3.  **Adjust Backend `tsconfig.json`**:\n    Since we are using `ts-node` (via `nodemon` in `dev:server`) and `vite` (which handles TS via esbuild), we generally prefer resolving to **source** files during development to avoid the need for constant `tsc -b` rebuilding.\n    The error `TS6305: Output file ... has not been built` is specific to Project References.\n    \n    I will try to **remove the `references`** section from `backend/tsconfig.json`. We already have `paths` mappings pointing to the source code. If we remove `references`, TypeScript will treat the imported code as part of the current compilation (via the `paths` alias) rather than a pre-built library dependency. This is usually smoother for active development in a monorepo unless the codebase is massive.\n\n    *Alternative*: Keep references but ensure `npm run build` is run for packages before starting dev. The error log shows `npm error command sh -c npx prisma generate && tsc --build` failed. This suggests the build itself is broken.\n\nLet's look at the errors again:\n`src/lib/analysis/HotspotDetector.ts:7:8 - error TS6305: Output file '/.../packages/types/dist/index.d.ts' has not been built from source file '/.../packages/types/src/index.ts'.`\n\nThis confirms `tsc --build` is being run and failing because it thinks the types package isn't built.\n\nI will start by fixing the `package.json` exports, as that is definitely wrong for modern node resolution. Then I will modify the backend `tsconfig.json` to rely on path mappings (Source) rather than Project References (Dist) for a smoother dev experience.\n\n```jsonl\n{\"type\": \"function_call_start\", "I have addressed the build errors by fixing the package exports and adjusting the backend TypeScript configuration.\n\n**Root Cause:**\n1.  **Missing Export Types:** The `package.json` files for `@goodnumbers/types` and `@goodnumbers/schemas` were missing the `types` condition in their `exports` field. This prevents modern resolution (like `bundler` or `node16`) from finding the declaration files.\n2.  **Conflicting Config:** The backend `tsconfig.json` was using **Project References** (`references`) *and* **Path Aliases** (`paths`) simultaneously.\n    *   `references` tells `tsc` to look for built `.d.ts` files in the `dist` folder of dependencies.\n    *   `paths` tells `tsc` to look at the raw `src` files.\n    *   When both are present, `tsc` prioritizes the Project Reference but fails if the dependency hasn't been built yet (hence the `TS6305` error).\n\n**Changes Applied:**\n1.  **Updated `packages/types/package.json`**: Added `\"types\": \"./dist/index.d.ts\"` to the exports.\n2.  **Updated `packages/schemas/package.json`**: Added `\"types\": \"./dist/index.d.ts\"` to the exports.\n3.  **Updated `backend/tsconfig.json`**: Removed the `references` section.\n    *   **Why?** By removing strict project references, we force TypeScript to rely solely on the `paths` mappings. This means the backend will compile against the *source code* of your packages (`src/index.ts`) rather than requiring them to be pre-built in `dist/`.\n    *   **Benefit:** This is much better for development (`dev` mode) because changes in `packages/` are immediately picked up by the backend without needing a separate build step for every package change.\n\n**Next Steps:**\nPlease try running your dev command again. The errors regarding `TS6305` and `TS2307` should be resolved.\n\n```bash\njust dev\n```"

"<function_result call_id=\"1\">\nexport type Json =\n  | string\n  | number\n  | boolean\n  | null\n  | { [key: string]: Json }\n  | Json[];\n\nexport interface JournalStatus {\n  status: string;\n  progress: number;\n  statusMessage: string | null;\n}\n\nexport interface GlucoseEntry {\n  sgv: number;\n  date: number; // Timestamp\n  dateString?: string; // Optional ISO string if available\n}\n\n</function_result>\n\n<function_result call_id=\"2\">\nimport { DateTime, Info } from 'luxon';\nimport { v4 as uuidv4 } from 'uuid';\nimport {\n  GlycemicEvent,\n  GlycemicCluster,\n  GlucoseEntry,\n} from '@goodnumbers/types';\n\nconst MIN_DURATION_MINUTES = 20;\nconst MAX_ENTRIES = 5000;\nexport const BUFFER_MINUTES = 180;\n\nexport class HotspotDetector {\n  private timezone: string;\n\n  constructor(timezone: string) {\n    if (Info.isValidIANAZone(timezone)) {\n      this.timezone = timezone;\n    } else {\n      console.warn(\n        `[HotspotDetector] Invalid timezone '${timezone}' provided. Defaulting to UTC.`,\n      );\n      this.timezone = 'UTC';\n    }\n  }\n\n  public detectEvents(\n    entries: GlucoseEntry[],\n    type: 'hyper' | 'hypo',\n    threshold: number,\n  ): GlycemicEvent[] {\n    const events: GlycemicEvent[] = [];\n    let startIndex = -1;\n\n    // 0. Security: Limit input size to prevent DoS\n    const limitedEntries = entries.slice(0, MAX_ENTRIES);\n\n    // 1. Sort chronologically by timestamp\n    const sorted = [...limitedEntries].sort((a, b) => a.date - b.date);\n\n    for (let i = 0; i < sorted.length; i++) {\n      const entry = sorted[i];\n      const isTrigger =\n        type === 'hyper' ? entry.sgv >= threshold : entry.sgv <= threshold;\n\n      if (isTrigger) {\n        if (startIndex === -1) {\n          startIndex = i;\n        }\n      } else {\n        if (startIndex !== -1) {\n          // Sequence ended at i - 1\n          this.processSequence(sorted, startIndex, i, events, type);\n          startIndex = -1;\n        }\n      }\n    }\n    // Check tail\n    if (startIndex !== -1) {\n      this.processSequence(sorted, startIndex, sorted.length, events, type);\n    }\n\n    return events;\n  }\n\n  private processSequence(\n    allEntries: GlucoseEntry[],\n    startIndex: number,\n    endIndex: number,\n    events: GlycemicEvent[],\n    type: 'hyper' | 'hypo',\n  ) {\n    // Core event entries (exclusive of endIndex)\n    const seq = allEntries.slice(startIndex, endIndex);\n    if (seq.length === 0) return;\n\n    const first = seq[0];\n    const last = seq[seq.length - 1];\n\n    // Use dateString if available, otherwise convert timestamp\n    const firstDate = first.dateString || new Date(first.date).toISOString();\n    const lastDate = last.dateString || new Date(last.date).toISOString();\n\n    const startTime = DateTime.fromISO(firstDate).setZone(this.timezone);\n    const endTime = DateTime.fromISO(lastDate).setZone(this.timezone);\n\n    // Calculate duration in minutes\n    const duration = endTime.diff(startTime, 'minutes').minutes;\n\n    if (duration >= MIN_DURATION_MINUTES) {\n      // --- Calculate Buffer Indices ---\n      const bufferStartTime = startTime.minus({ minutes: BUFFER_MINUTES });\n      const bufferEndTime = endTime.plus({ minutes: BUFFER_MINUTES });\n\n      // Scan backwards for start buffer\n      let bufferStartIndex = startIndex;\n      while (bufferStartIndex > 0) {\n        const prevEntry = allEntries[bufferStartIndex - 1];\n        const prevTime = DateTime.fromMillis(prevEntry.date).setZone(\n          this.timezone,\n        );\n        if (prevTime < bufferStartTime) break;\n        bufferStartIndex--;\n      }\n\n      // Scan forwards for end buffer\n      let bufferEndIndex = endIndex;\n      while (bufferEndIndex < allEntries.length) {\n        const nextEntry = allEntries[bufferEndIndex];\n        const nextTime = DateTime.fromMillis(nextEntry.date).setZone(\n          this.timezone,\n        );\n        if (nextTime > bufferEndTime) break;\n        bufferEndIndex++;\n      }\n\n      // Extract extended readings\n      const extendedReadings = allEntries\n        .slice(bufferStartIndex, bufferEndIndex)\n        .map((e) => ({\n          timestamp: e.dateString || new Date(e.date).toISOString(),\n          value: e.sgv,\n        }));\n\n      events.push({\n        id: uuidv4(),\n        type,\n        startTime: startTime.toISO()!,\n        endTime: endTime.toISO()!,\n        startMinuteOfDay: startTime.hour * 60 + startTime.minute,\n        durationMinutes: duration,\n        readings: extendedReadings,\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  public findClusters(events: GlycemicEvent[]): GlycemicCluster[] {\n    const visited = new Set<string>();\n    const clusters: GlycemicCluster[] = [];\n\n    for (const event of events) {\n      if (visited.has(event.id)) continue;\n\n      // DFS to find connected component\n      const component: GlycemicEvent[] = [];\n      const stack = [event];\n      visited.add(event.id);\n\n      while (stack.length > 0) {\n        const current = stack.pop()!;\n        component.push(current);\n\n        for (const other of events) {\n          if (!visited.has(other.id) && this.doEventsOverlap(current, other)) {\n            visited.add(other.id);\n            stack.push(other);\n          }\n        }\n      }\n\n      // Filter: Must have >= 3 distinct days\n      const distinctDays = new Set(\n        component.map(\n          (e) => DateTime.fromISO(e.startTime).setZone(this.timezone).weekday,\n        ),\n      );\n\n      if (distinctDays.size >= 2) {\n        clusters.push(\n          this.buildClusterObject(component, Array.from(distinctDays)),\n        );\n      }\n    }\n    return clusters;\n  }\n\n  private buildClusterObject(\n    events: GlycemicEvent[],\n    activeDays: number[],\n  ): GlycemicCluster {\n    const type = events[0].type;\n    const avgDuration =\n      events.reduce((sum, e) => sum + e.durationMinutes, 0) / events.length;\n\n    // Circular Mean for start times\n    let sinSum = 0;\n    let cosSum = 0;\n    for (const e of events) {\n      const angle = (e.startMinuteOfDay / 1440) * 2 * Math.PI;\n      sinSum += Math.sin(angle);\n      cosSum += Math.cos(angle);\n    }\n    const avgAngle = Math.atan2(sinSum, cosSum);\n    let avgStartMinute = (avgAngle / (2 * Math.PI)) * 1440;\n    if (avgStartMinute < 0) avgStartMinute += 1440;\n\n    return {\n      id: uuidv4(),\n      type,\n      avgStartMinute: Math.round(avgStartMinute),\n      avgDurationMinutes: Math.round(avgDuration),\n      eventCount: events.length,\n      activeDays: activeDays.sort((a, b) => a - b),\n      events,\n    };\n  }\n\n  private doEventsOverlap(a: GlycemicEvent, b: GlycemicEvent): boolean {\n    const buffer = 15; // minutes\n    const aIntervals = this.getNormalizedIntervals(a);\n    const bIntervals = this.getNormalizedIntervals(b);\n\n    return aIntervals.some((intA) =>\n      bIntervals.some(\n        (intB) =>\n          // Check overlap with buffer\n          Math.max(intA.start, intB.start) <\n          Math.min(intA.end, intB.end) + buffer,\n      ),\n    );\n  }\n\n  private getNormalizedIntervals(\n    e: GlycemicEvent,\n  ): { start: number; end: number }[] {\n    const start = e.startMinuteOfDay;\n    const end = (start + e.durationMinutes) % 1440;\n\n    // If the event wraps around midnight (start + duration >= 1440)\n    // Or if it ends exactly at midnight (which is 0 in modulo but 1440 in continuous time)\n    // We treat it as two intervals: [start, 1440] and [0, end]\n    if (start + e.durationMinutes >= 1440) {\n      return [\n        { start, end: 1440 },\n        { start: 0, end },\n      ];\n    }\n    return [{ start, end: start + e.durationMinutes }];\n  }\n}\n\n</function_result>"
